People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Just... what I mentioned a moment ago. I suppose I'm just a bit slower. I still feel like I failed her somehow.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I... well, I try to keep up with things. I suppose not.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes, a little. It just feels a bit like my body is moving through thick mud, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or restless, or do you feel like you're slowing down in your movements compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: I don't think I'm restless, no. Just... what I mentioned a moment ago. I suppose I'm just a bit slower. I haven't really noticed much else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: I suppose... well, yes. I do think about it quite a bit. The last few weeks with her. I just keep going over it in my head, telling myself I should have called more often. Or... or just listened better. Done something more useful. I know, in my head, that I couldn't have changed what happened, but... I still feel like I failed her somehow.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I... well, I try to keep up with things. I still make myself show up for the book club, or... the dance night. But I don't know, lately I just sit there feeling a bit like I'm behind a pane of glass. And... well, even my garden, or cooking for the neighbors... it just doesn't give me that little lift it used to. So... I suppose not.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes, a little. I've noticed I'll just stare at the same budgeting spreadsheet for a while. Or... if I'm reading something for a workshop, I might reread a paragraph three or four times. And I still won't really take in what it says. It just doesn't seem to stick.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble getting started with activities?\n\nPatient: I suppose... yes. I just... I get worn out very easily lately. Even just running simple errands, or... trying to get the papers together for the Cultural Center. It feels a bit like... well, like my body is just moving through thick mud. It takes a lot out of me to get started.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of sadness and emptiness every day since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: Yes... I suppose so. It's been about a month now. Since she... well, since she died. It was very unexpected, so... yes, pretty much every day since then.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not... not really, no. I mean, I still make myself show up for things. The book club, or... the dance night. But I just sort of sit there. It feels a bit like I'm... behind a pane of glass, I suppose. Even my garden... or cooking for the neighbors. It just doesn't give me that little lift it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Well... like I just mentioned, I suppose it's mostly those things. The gardening, and... cooking for people. I used to really look forward to them. Now... I don't know. I just do them out of habit, but... that feeling of enjoyment just isn't there anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently difficult every day?\n\nPatient: I suppose... no, not really. It's just been... consistently like this. Every day.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just mentioned this a moment ago. No, not really. Like I said, I still make myself go to the book club and the dance night, but... I just sit there feeling like I'm behind a pane of glass. And... even the gardening, or cooking for the neighbors... they just don't give me that lift they used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead, rather than just not waking up?\n\nPatient: Well... no. Not exactly that I'd be better off dead. I don't... I don't have any plan to do anything like that. But... I suppose what finally made me come in here is... well, there are mornings where I just think... it might be easier if I just didn't wake up at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety since your sister passed away?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I haven't really felt restless or anything like that. I mostly just... sit quietly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
